tbmm-2018-12-20sit01spe0015par0112	Mr. President, I have made a pledge on the 14th amendment of the budget bill. Along with the new Constitution, which literally led to a regime change, the budget processes that have already failed and been undermined have completely deteriorated. It began with the Ministry of Development ahead of the new constitution, changing budget production processes with the Finance Ministry, Parliamentary Plan Budget Commission and the Parliamentary General Assembly, becoming a budget law proposal submitted by the presidency. The new system offers a budget law proposal prepared by the President to parliament. In Article 161 of the existing Constitution, if the budget law is not implemented during the duration of the budget law, there will be a temporary budget law. If the temporary budget law is not adopted, the budget of the previous year will be applied according to the rate of revaluement until the new budget law is adopted. Members of the Grand National Assembly of Turkey state their opinion on public administration budgets during the discussion of each budget, they cannot offer extenuating costs or reducing revenues. Their statements are included. As you can see from the relevant clause, parliament has no right to make any changes in its budget proposals. So the offer has to be accepted or rejected. Because budgets are in a sense the House's confidence in governments, rejection is an invitation to resign as a political executive. However, even if the budget is rejected in the new system in Turkey, the grants are implemented by increasing the growth rates of the previous year. Since the Council of Ministers or the government is not in the new system, there is no government to resign. Thus, the operating mechanism for the new system's budget law is official evidence that this right has been taken from the people who have the budgetary rights from 1215 to today. In a sense, we're on an antidemocratic axis in Turkey where the MPs that the people gave their power of attorney through the elections were rendered unfunctional by constitutional binding. tbmm-2018-12-20sit04spe0644par0319	Thank you. Mr. President, I have made a pledge on behalf of the CHP Group on the 5th article entitled State debt. I salute the General Assembly. To understand the economic situation we're in today, I'd like to start a little bit behind. Dear MPs, the Republic of Turkey has begun its life with a huge debt that Ottomans left it. The Ottoman state was in debt in large amounts and heavy conditions until 1875-1876, when it declared it could not continue its debt payments without its first debt in 1854. After 1860, there was more debt than $2 British lira for every 1 British lira that entered the treasury. Compared to the debt of other states from international financial markets, the Ottoman state was in debt under extremely negative circumstances, and in these circumstances, the state used a large portion of the funds for current expenditures, it built palaces in Boğaziçi, but it didn't spare any funds for its investments. The main reason for the financial debt of the Ottoman State is that the central state cannot find a long-term solution to the financial crisis and cannot prevent budget deficits.
